           │ > The alternating bit protocol (ABP) is a protocol for       │
           │ transmitting messages along a 'lossy line,' i.e., a line     │
           │ which may lose or duplicate messages. The protocol           │
           │ guarantees that, providing the line doesn't lose infinitely  │
           │ many messages, communication between the sender and the      │
           │ receiver will be successful. (We allow the line to lose or   │
           │ duplicate messages, but it may not corrupt messages;         │
           │ however, there is no way of guaranteeing successful          │
           │ transmission along a line which can corrupt.) The ABP works  │
           │ as follows. There are four entities, or agents: the sender,  │
           │ the receiver, the message channel and the acknowledgement    │
           │ channel. The sender transmits the first part of the message  │
           │ together with the 'control' bit 0. If, and when, the         │
           │ receiver receives a message with the control bit 0, it sends │
           │ 0 along the acknowledgement channel. When the sender         │
           │ receives this acknowledgement, it sends the next packet with │
           │ the control bit 1. If and when the receiver receives this,   │
           │ it acknowledges by sending a 1 on the acknowledgement        │
           │ channel. By alternating the control bit, both receiver and   │
           │ sender can guard against duplicating messages and losing     │
           │ messages (i.e., they ignore messages that have the           │
           │ unexpected control bit). If the sender doesn't get the       │
           │ expected acknowledgement, it continually re-sends the        │

           │ doesn't get a message with the expected control bit, it      │

           │ The alternating bit protocol (ABP) is a protocol for         │
           │ transmitting messages along a 'lossy line,' i.e., a line     │
           │ which may lose or duplicate messages. The protocol           │
           │ guarantees that, providing the line doesn't lose infinitely  │
           │ many messages, communication between the sender and the      │
           │ receiver will be successful. (We allow the line to lose or   │
           │ duplicate messages, but it may not corrupt messages;         │
           │ however, there is no way of guaranteeing successful          │
           │ transmission along a line which can corrupt.)                │
           │                                                              │
           │ The ABP works as follows. There are four entities, or        │
           │ agents: the sender, the receiver, the message channel and    │
           │ the acknowledgement channel. The sender transmits the first  │
           │ part of the message together with the 'control' bit 0. If,   │
           │ and when, the receiver receives a message with the control   │
           │ bit 0, it sends 0 along the acknowledgement channel. When    │
           │ the sender receives this acknowledgement, it sends the next  │
           │ packet with the control bit 1. If and when the receiver      │
           │ receives this, it acknowledges by sending a 1 on the         │
           │ acknowledgement channel. By alternating the control bit,     │
           │ both receiver and sender can guard against duplicating       │
           │ messages and losing messages (i.e., they ignore messages     │

           │ Make sure that your code extends the `Module` class below    │

           │                                                              │
           │ > The alternating bit protocol (ABP) is a protocol for       │
           │ transmitting messages along a 'lossy line,' i.e., a line     │
           │ which may lose or duplicate messages. The protocol           │
           │ guarantees that, providing the line doesn't lose infinitely  │
           │ many messages, communication between the sender and the      │
           │ receiver will be successful. (We allow the line to lose or   │
           │ duplicate messages, but it may not corrupt messages;         │
           │ however, there is no way of guaranteeing successful          │
           │ transmission along a line which can corrupt.) The ABP works  │
           │ as follows. There are four entities, or agents: the sender,  │
           │ the receiver, the message channel and the acknowledgement    │
           │ channel. The sender transmits the first part of the message  │
           │ together with the 'control' bit 0. If, and when, the         │
           │ receiver receives a message with the control bit 0, it sends │
           │ 0 along the acknowledgement channel. When the sender         │
           │ receives this acknowledgement, it sends the next packet with │
           │ the control bit 1. If and when the receiver receives this,   │
           │ it acknowledges by sending a 1 on the acknowledgement        │
           │ channel. By alternating the control bit, both receiver and   │
           │ sender can guard against duplicating messages and losing     │
           │ messages (i.e., they ignore messages that have the           │
           │ unexpected control bit). If the sender doesn't get the       │
           │ expected acknowledgement, it continually re-sends the        │
           │ message, until the acknowledgement arrives. If the receiver  │
           │ doesn't get a message with the expected control bit, it      │
           │ continually resends the previous acknowledgement. Write a    │
           │ model for the alternating bit protocol. Write a set of       │
           │ properties that check that your model behaves as described   │
           │ above. Use the variable names st, message1, message2 and     │
           │ ack.                                                         │

           │         pass                                                 │

           │         implementation defines an instance of M called m,    │

           │                                                              │
           │ > The alternating bit protocol (ABP) is a protocol for       │
           │ transmitting messages along a 'lossy line,' i.e., a line     │
           │ which may lose or duplicate messages. The protocol           │
           │ guarantees that, providing the line doesn't lose infinitely  │
           │ many messages, communication between the sender and the      │
           │ receiver will be successful. (We allow the line to lose or   │
           │ duplicate messages, but it may not corrupt messages;         │
           │ however, there is no way of guaranteeing successful          │
           │ transmission along a line which can corrupt.) The ABP works  │
           │ as follows. There are four entities, or agents: the sender,  │
           │ the receiver, the message channel and the acknowledgement    │
           │ channel. The sender transmits the first part of the message  │
           │ together with the 'control' bit 0. If, and when, the         │
           │ receiver receives a message with the control bit 0, it sends │
           │ 0 along the acknowledgement channel. When the sender         │
           │ receives this acknowledgement, it sends the next packet with │
           │ the control bit 1. If and when the receiver receives this,   │
           │ it acknowledges by sending a 1 on the acknowledgement        │
           │ channel. By alternating the control bit, both receiver and   │
           │ sender can guard against duplicating messages and losing     │
           │ messages (i.e., they ignore messages that have the           │
           │ unexpected control bit). If the sender doesn't get the       │
           │ expected acknowledgement, it continually re-sends the        │
           │ message, until the acknowledgement arrives. If the receiver  │
           │ doesn't get a message with the expected control bit, it      │
           │ continually resends the previous acknowledgement. Write a    │
           │ model for the alternating bit protocol. Write a set of       │
           │ properties that check that your model behaves as described   │
           │ above. Use the variable names st, message1, message2 and     │
           │ ack.                                                         │

           │                                                              │
           │ > The alternating bit protocol (ABP) is a protocol for       │
           │ transmitting messages along a 'lossy line,' i.e., a line     │
           │ which may lose or duplicate messages. The protocol           │
           │ guarantees that, providing the line doesn't lose infinitely  │
           │ many messages, communication between the sender and the      │
           │ receiver will be successful. (We allow the line to lose or   │
           │ duplicate messages, but it may not corrupt messages;         │
           │ however, there is no way of guaranteeing successful          │
           │ transmission along a line which can corrupt.) The ABP works  │
           │ as follows. There are four entities, or agents: the sender,  │
           │ the receiver, the message channel and the acknowledgement    │
           │ channel. The sender transmits the first part of the message  │
           │ together with the 'control' bit 0. If, and when, the         │
           │ receiver receives a message with the control bit 0, it sends │
           │ 0 along the acknowledgement channel. When the sender         │
           │ receives this acknowledgement, it sends the next packet with │
           │ the control bit 1. If and when the receiver receives this,   │
           │ it acknowledges by sending a 1 on the acknowledgement        │
           │ channel. By alternating the control bit, both receiver and   │
           │ sender can guard against duplicating messages and losing     │
           │ messages (i.e., they ignore messages that have the           │
           │ unexpected control bit). If the sender doesn't get the       │
           │ expected acknowledgement, it continually re-sends the        │
           │ message, until the acknowledgement arrives. If the receiver  │
           │ doesn't get a message with the expected control bit, it      │
           │ continually resends the previous acknowledgement. Write a    │
           │ model for the alternating bit protocol. Write a set of       │
           │ properties that check that your model behaves as described   │
           │ above. Use the variable names st, message1, message2 and     │
           │ ack.                                                         │

           │ ORIGINAL TASK DESCRIPTION:                                   │
           │ The alternating bit protocol (ABP) is a protocol for         │
           │ transmitting messages along a 'lossy line,' i.e., a line     │
           │ which may lose or duplicate messages. The protocol           │
           │ guarantees that, providing the line doesn't lose infinitely  │
           │ many messages, communication between the sender and the      │
           │ receiver will be successful. (We allow the line to lose or   │
           │ duplicate messages, but it may not corrupt messages;         │
           │ however, there is no way of guaranteeing successful          │
           │ transmission along a line which can corrupt.)                │
           │                                                              │
           │ The ABP works as follows. There are four entities, or        │
           │ agents: the sender, the receiver, the message channel and    │
           │ the acknowledgement channel. The sender transmits the first  │
           │ part of the message together with the 'control' bit 0. If,   │
           │ and when, the receiver receives a message with the control   │
           │ bit 0, it sends 0 along the acknowledgement channel. When    │
           │ the sender receives this acknowledgement, it sends the next  │
           │ packet with the control bit 1. If and when the receiver      │
           │ receives this, it acknowledges by sending a 1 on the         │
           │ acknowledgement channel. By alternating the control bit,     │
           │ both receiver and sender can guard against duplicating       │
           │ messages and losing messages (i.e., they ignore messages     │
           │ that have the unexpected control bit).                       │
           │ If the sender doesn't get the expected acknowledgement, it   │
           │ continually re-sends the message, until the acknowledgement  │
           │ arrives. If the receiver doesn't get a message with the      │
           │ expected control bit, it continually resends the previous    │
           │ acknowledgement.                                             │
           │                                                              │

           │ > The alternating bit protocol (ABP) is a protocol for       │
           │ transmitting messages along a 'lossy line,' i.e., a line     │
           │ which may lose or duplicate messages. The protocol           │
           │ guarantees that, providing the line doesn't lose infinitely  │
           │ many messages, communication between the sender and the      │
           │ receiver will be successful. (We allow the line to lose or   │
           │ duplicate messages, but it may not corrupt messages;         │
           │ however, there is no way of guaranteeing successful          │
           │ transmission along a line which can corrupt.) The ABP works  │
           │ as follows. There are four entities, or agents: the sender,  │
           │ the receiver, the message channel and the acknowledgement    │
           │ channel. The sender transmits the first part of the message  │
           │ together with the 'control' bit 0. If, and when, the         │
           │ receiver receives a message with the control bit 0, it sends │
           │ 0 along the acknowledgement channel. When the sender         │
           │ receives this acknowledgement, it sends the next packet with │
           │ the control bit 1. If and when the receiver receives this,   │
           │ it acknowledges by sending a 1 on the acknowledgement        │
           │ channel. By alternating the control bit, both receiver and   │
           │ sender can guard against duplicating messages and losing     │
           │ messages (i.e., they ignore messages that have the           │
           │ unexpected control bit). If the sender doesn't get the       │
           │ expected acknowledgement, it continually re-sends the        │
           │ message, until the acknowledgement arrives. If the receiver  │
           │ doesn't get a message with the expected control bit, it      │
           │ continually resends the previous acknowledgement. Write a    │
           │ model for the alternating bit protocol. Write a set of       │
           │ properties that check that your model behaves as described   │
           │ above. Use the variable names st, message1, message2 and     │
           │ ack.                                                         │

           │ and relates their                                            │

           │ variables. Every instance                                    │
           │         variable must be related to a local variable. For    │
           │ example, let M be                                            │
           │         another module with inputs x and y, and output z.    │
           │ The following                                                │
           │         implementation defines an instance of M called m,    │
           │ and connects M's                                             │
           │         input variable x to the local variable self.a, M's   │
           │ input variable y to                                          │
           │         the local variable self.b, and M's output variable z │

           │ The alternating bit protocol (ABP) is a protocol for         │
           │ transmitting messages along a 'lossy line,' i.e., a line     │
           │ which may lose or duplicate messages. The protocol           │
           │ guarantees that, providing the line doesn't lose infinitely  │
           │ many messages, communication between the sender and the      │
           │ receiver will be successful. (We allow the line to lose or   │
           │ duplicate messages, but it may not corrupt messages;         │
           │ however, there is no way of guaranteeing successful          │
           │ transmission along a line which can corrupt.)                │
           │                                                              │
           │ The ABP works as follows. There are four entities, or        │
           │ agents: the sender, the receiver, the message channel and    │
           │ the acknowledgement channel. The sender transmits the first  │
           │ part of the message together with the 'control' bit 0. If,   │
           │ and when, the receiver receives a message with the control   │
           │ bit 0, it sends 0 along the acknowledgement channel. When    │
           │ the sender receives this acknowledgement, it sends the next  │
           │ packet with the control bit 1. If and when the receiver      │
           │ receives this, it acknowledges by sending a 1 on the         │
           │ acknowledgement channel. By alternating the control bit,     │
           │ both receiver and sender can guard against duplicating       │
           │ messages and losing messages (i.e., they ignore messages     │
           │ that have the unexpected control bit).                       │
           │ If the sender doesn't get the expected acknowledgement, it   │
           │ continually re-sends the message, until the acknowledgement  │
           │ arrives. If the receiver doesn't get a message with the      │
           │ expected control bit, it continually resends the previous    │

           │                                                              │
           │ > The alternating bit protocol (ABP) is a protocol for       │
           │ transmitting messages along a 'lossy line,' i.e., a line     │
           │ which may lose or duplicate messages. The protocol           │
           │ guarantees that, providing the line doesn't lose infinitely  │
           │ many messages, communication between the sender and the      │
           │ receiver will be successful. (We allow the line to lose or   │
           │ duplicate messages, but it may not corrupt messages;         │
           │ however, there is no way of guaranteeing successful          │
           │ transmission along a line which can corrupt.) The ABP works  │
           │ as follows. There are four entities, or agents: the sender,  │
           │ the receiver, the message channel and the acknowledgement    │
           │ channel. The sender transmits the first part of the message  │
           │ together with the 'control' bit 0. If, and when, the         │
           │ receiver receives a message with the control bit 0, it sends │
           │ 0 along the acknowledgement channel. When the sender         │
           │ receives this acknowledgement, it sends the next packet with │
           │ the control bit 1. If and when the receiver receives this,   │
           │ it acknowledges by sending a 1 on the acknowledgement        │
           │ channel. By alternating the control bit, both receiver and   │
           │ sender can guard against duplicating messages and losing     │
           │ messages (i.e., they ignore messages that have the           │
           │ unexpected control bit). If the sender doesn't get the       │
           │ expected acknowledgement, it continually re-sends the        │
           │ message, until the acknowledgement arrives. If the receiver  │
           │ doesn't get a message with the expected control bit, it      │
           │ continually resends the previous acknowledgement. Write a    │
           │ model for the alternating bit protocol. Write a set of       │
           │ properties that check that your model behaves as described   │
           │ above. Use the variable names st, message1, message2 and     │
           │ ack.                                                         │

           │             if self.z:                                       │

           │ > The alternating bit protocol (ABP) is a protocol for       │
           │ transmitting messages along a 'lossy line,' i.e., a line     │
           │ which may lose or duplicate messages. The protocol           │
           │ guarantees that, providing the line doesn't lose infinitely  │
           │ many messages, communication between the sender and the      │
           │ receiver will be successful. (We allow the line to lose or   │
           │ duplicate messages, but it may not corrupt messages;         │
           │ however, there is no way of guaranteeing successful          │
           │ transmission along a line which can corrupt.) The ABP works  │
           │ as follows. There are four entities, or agents: the sender,  │
           │ the receiver, the message channel and the acknowledgement    │
           │ channel. The sender transmits the first part of the message  │
           │ together with the 'control' bit 0. If, and when, the         │
           │ receiver receives a message with the control bit 0, it sends │
           │ 0 along the acknowledgement channel. When the sender         │
           │ receives this acknowledgement, it sends the next packet with │
           │ the control bit 1. If and when the receiver receives this,   │
           │ it acknowledges by sending a 1 on the acknowledgement        │
           │ channel. By alternating the control bit, both receiver and   │
           │ sender can guard against duplicating messages and losing     │
           │ messages (i.e., they ignore messages that have the           │
           │ unexpected control bit). If the sender doesn't get the       │
           │ expected acknowledgement, it continually re-sends the        │
           │ message, until the acknowledgement arrives. If the receiver  │
           │ doesn't get a message with the expected control bit, it      │
           │ continually resends the previous acknowledgement. Write a    │
           │ model for the alternating bit protocol. Write a set of       │

           │                                                              │
           │ > The alternating bit protocol (ABP) is a protocol for       │
           │ transmitting messages along a 'lossy line,' i.e., a line     │
           │ which may lose or duplicate messages. The protocol           │
           │ guarantees that, providing the line doesn't lose infinitely  │
           │ many messages, communication between the sender and the      │
           │ receiver will be successful. (We allow the line to lose or   │
           │ duplicate messages, but it may not corrupt messages;         │
           │ however, there is no way of guaranteeing successful          │
           │ transmission along a line which can corrupt.) The ABP works  │
           │ as follows. There are four entities, or agents: the sender,  │
           │ the receiver, the message channel and the acknowledgement    │
           │ channel. The sender transmits the first part of the message  │
           │ together with the 'control' bit 0. If, and when, the         │
           │ receiver receives a message with the control bit 0, it sends │
           │ 0 along the acknowledgement channel. When the sender         │
           │ receives this acknowledgement, it sends the next packet with │
           │ the control bit 1. If and when the receiver receives this,   │
           │ it acknowledges by sending a 1 on the acknowledgement        │
           │ channel. By alternating the control bit, both receiver and   │
           │ sender can guard against duplicating messages and losing     │
           │ messages (i.e., they ignore messages that have the           │
           │ unexpected control bit). If the sender doesn't get the       │
           │ expected acknowledgement, it continually re-sends the        │
           │ message, until the acknowledgement arrives. If the receiver  │
           │ doesn't get a message with the expected control bit, it      │
           │ continually resends the previous acknowledgement. Write a    │
           │ model for the alternating bit protocol. Write a set of       │
           │ properties that check that your model behaves as described   │
           │ above. Use the variable names st, message1, message2 and     │
           │ ack.                                                         │

           │ The alternating bit protocol (ABP) is a protocol for         │
           │ transmitting messages along a 'lossy line,' i.e., a line     │
           │ which may lose or duplicate messages. The protocol           │
           │ guarantees that, providing the line doesn't lose infinitely  │
           │ many messages, communication between the sender and the      │
           │ receiver will be successful. (We allow the line to lose or   │
           │ duplicate messages, but it may not corrupt messages;         │
           │ however, there is no way of guaranteeing successful          │
           │ transmission along a line which can corrupt.)                │
           │                                                              │
           │ The ABP works as follows. There are four entities, or        │
           │ agents: the sender, the receiver, the message channel and    │
           │ the acknowledgement channel. The sender transmits the first  │
           │ part of the message together with the 'control' bit 0. If,   │
           │ and when, the receiver receives a message with the control   │
           │ bit 0, it sends 0 along the acknowledgement channel. When    │
           │ the sender receives this acknowledgement, it sends the next  │
           │ packet with the control bit 1. If and when the receiver      │
           │ receives this, it acknowledges by sending a 1 on the         │
           │ acknowledgement channel. By alternating the control bit,     │
           │ both receiver and sender can guard against duplicating       │
           │ messages and losing messages (i.e., they ignore messages     │
           │ that have the unexpected control bit).                       │
           │ If the sender doesn't get the expected acknowledgement, it   │
           │ continually re-sends the message, until the acknowledgement  │
           │ arrives. If the receiver doesn't get a message with the      │
           │ expected control bit, it continually resends the previous    │
           │ acknowledgement.                                             │
           │                                                              │
           │ Write a model for the alternating bit protocol. Write a set  │

           │ described above.                                             │

           │                                                              │
           │ > The alternating bit protocol (ABP) is a protocol for       │
           │ transmitting messages along a 'lossy line,' i.e., a line     │
           │ which may lose or duplicate messages. The protocol           │
           │ guarantees that, providing the line doesn't lose infinitely  │
           │ many messages, communication between the sender and the      │
           │ receiver will be successful. (We allow the line to lose or   │
           │ duplicate messages, but it may not corrupt messages;         │
           │ however, there is no way of guaranteeing successful          │
           │ transmission along a line which can corrupt.) The ABP works  │
           │ as follows. There are four entities, or agents: the sender,  │
           │ the receiver, the message channel and the acknowledgement    │
           │ channel. The sender transmits the first part of the message  │
           │ together with the 'control' bit 0. If, and when, the         │
           │ receiver receives a message with the control bit 0, it sends │
           │ 0 along the acknowledgement channel. When the sender         │
           │ receives this acknowledgement, it sends the next packet with │
           │ the control bit 1. If and when the receiver receives this,   │
           │ it acknowledges by sending a 1 on the acknowledgement        │
           │ channel. By alternating the control bit, both receiver and   │
           │ sender can guard against duplicating messages and losing     │
           │ messages (i.e., they ignore messages that have the           │
           │ unexpected control bit). If the sender doesn't get the       │
           │ expected acknowledgement, it continually re-sends the        │
           │ message, until the acknowledgement arrives. If the receiver  │
           │ doesn't get a message with the expected control bit, it      │
           │ continually resends the previous acknowledgement. Write a    │
           │ model for the alternating bit protocol. Write a set of       │
           │ properties that check that your model behaves as described   │
           │ above. Use the variable names st, message1, message2 and     │

           │ ORIGINAL TASK DESCRIPTION:                                   │
           │ The alternating bit protocol (ABP) is a protocol for         │
           │ transmitting messages along a 'lossy line,' i.e., a line     │
           │ which may lose or duplicate messages. The protocol           │
           │ guarantees that, providing the line doesn't lose infinitely  │
           │ many messages, communication between the sender and the      │
           │ receiver will be successful. (We allow the line to lose or   │
           │ duplicate messages, but it may not corrupt messages;         │
           │ however, there is no way of guaranteeing successful          │
           │ transmission along a line which can corrupt.)                │
           │                                                              │
           │ The ABP works as follows. There are four entities, or        │
           │ agents: the sender, the receiver, the message channel and    │
           │ the acknowledgement channel. The sender transmits the first  │
           │ part of the message together with the 'control' bit 0. If,   │
           │ and when, the receiver receives a message with the control   │
           │ bit 0, it sends 0 along the acknowledgement channel. When    │
           │ the sender receives this acknowledgement, it sends the next  │
           │ packet with the control bit 1. If and when the receiver      │
           │ receives this, it acknowledges by sending a 1 on the         │
           │ acknowledgement channel. By alternating the control bit,     │
           │ both receiver and sender can guard against duplicating       │
           │ messages and losing messages (i.e., they ignore messages     │
           │ that have the unexpected control bit).                       │
           │ If the sender doesn't get the expected acknowledgement, it   │
           │ continually re-sends the message, until the acknowledgement  │
           │ arrives. If the receiver doesn't get a message with the      │
           │ expected control bit, it continually resends the previous    │
           │ acknowledgement.                                             │
           │                                                              │

           │ block: specification                                         │
           │ fix: The invariant for 'message1_inv' is incorrect since it  │
           │ tries to assert a condition that will always be false:       │
           │ '(self.message1 == (1 - self.message1))'. This condition     │
           │ implies both the current and next state of 'message1' should │
           │ be equal to each other, which is logically inconsistent when │
           │ toggling bits. The correct condition should verify that      │
           │ 'message1' toggles whenever 'st' is 0, which was intended    │
           │ but incorrectly expressed.                                   │
           │                                                              │

           │ statement '((self.ack == 0) and (self.ack == 1))' is         │
           │ logically contradictory as a bit cannot be both 0 and 1      │
           │ simultaneously. The correct invariant should ensure 'ack'    │
           │ acknowledges the correct state under given conditions where  │
           │ st and message align with expected values for ACK            │
           │ transitions.                                                 │
